- **`RT-SCHEDULE-PAUSE-END-AND-LAST-RESULT` — A schedule can be paused and resumed, ends after a number of runs or on a date, skips a run while the last one is still going, and remembers how its last run ended** · group: shared-work-v1 · `agent_runtime/work_store/schedule_store.py` · `agent_runtime/work_store/schedule_ops.py` · `agent_runtime/work_store/serve_scheduler.py` · `agent_runtime/serve_rpc/schedule.py` · found 2026-11-12 (read): a record has `enabled` but no verb sets it, no end condition, no result, and an overlapping fire is refused `already_taken` with nothing kept · the wire Eternia is built to: (1) `runtime.schedule.pause` / `runtime.schedule.resume` `{key}` answer `{schedule}`, flip `enabled`, and resume computes `next_fire` from now — also as `schedule_manage` ops; (2) `arm` takes `ends: {after_fires: <int ≥ 1> | null, until: <aware ISO time> | null}`, the record echoes `ends` and counts `fires` (fires that started a run), and a schedule that reaches its end is removed with a `schedule.ended` receipt; (3) `arm` takes `skip_while_running` (default true for `run_task`): a fire that finds the task's last run still live advances `next_fire`, counts nothing and records a `skipped` result; (4) the record carries `last_result: {verdict: done | blocked | stopped | failed | skipped | refused, at, detail, run_id}`, written when the fire's run settles or is refused; (5) `runtime.schedule.list`'s `supports` names each of `pause`, `ends`, `skip_while_running`, `last_result` it serves — Eternia shows each control only when its word is there · the seven fields are the owner's (2026-11-12, "all as reccomended is good") on `EterniaLauncher` row `COMP-WORK-V1-DECIDE-SCHEDULES` · DONE 2026-11-12 3c32e9d395 · result: runtime.schedule.pause/resume {key}; arm ends {after_fires, until} + skip_while_running; record echoes ends, fires, skip_while_running, last_result {verdict, at, detail, run_id}; schedule.ended / schedule.skipped receipts; list supports pause, ends, skip_while_running, last_result

- **`RT-BUILD-GONE-SILENT-SENDS-NO-NEW-FRAME` — A build that has gone silent shows as slowing, then stalled, in Background work without waiting for the next thing to happen** · group: shared-work-v1 · `agent_runtime/builds/sweep.py::BuildSweep.tick` · `agent_runtime/stream/frames.py::_is_running_work_event` · `agent_runtime/stream/fingerprint.py::_build_registry_parts` · found 2026-11-12 (launcher lanes w1112b-worklive live, w1112b-workfix read; launcher row `MC-ACTIVITY-STALLED-BUILD-NEVER-SHOWN`, evidence `EterniaLauncher/docs/stages/qa-reboot/live/2026-11-12/MC-QA-SHOT-L8-L9/w1112b-worklive/README.md`): an announced build whose writer froze (alive, heartbeat stopped) read "building" with `status=running` and the first frame's `issued_at` for 9 minutes, while `registry.evaluate` on the same record answered `stalled` (heartbeat age 549 s against 240 s). The row's `status` / `liveness` / `seconds_since_progress` are computed only when `running_work_frame` builds the projection, and a frame is built only on `process.started` / `process.ended` or a watchdog `state.reconciled` for `running_work`, whose fingerprint is the record file's mtime and size — a stall is no write, so no frame. The 30 s `BuildSweep.tick` re-evaluates every row but emits nothing when a row crosses to stalling or stalled (only an outcome change or the 900 s stall-fail, and its `build.ended` is not a running-work event either) · DONE 2026-11-12 b458fdcbca · handoff 2026-11-12: (w1112b-workfix) likely fix — `BuildSweep.tick` keeps each in-flight row's last wire status and, when it changes (running → stalling → stalled, and back on a new heartbeat), appends one event the stream reads as a running-work change (a `state.reconciled` with `families: ["running_work"]`, or a new type admitted by `_is_running_work_event`); check `build.ended` reaches the frame too. The launcher already draws "slowing" / "stalled · no heartbeat for Xm" from the row (`mission_activity_build_view.dart`) and computes no staleness itself, so no client change is needed; its row closes on a live picture once this lands. Test: a record with a frozen heartbeat, two sweep ticks across the threshold, one running-work event appended and the next frame's row `status: stalled` · result: the 30 s build sweep emits one build.liveness_changed event per in-flight wire-status change (running, stalling, stalled, and back); the stream re-issues the running_work section on it and on build.ended; stream goldens regenerated (decision_contract_hash only); tests/agent_runtime/test_build_sweep.

- **`RT-LATE-STEER-TURN-RUNS-AS-THE-WORK-REQUEST-IT-WAS-TYPED-AT` — A steer you typed at a task run that arrives too late runs next as your own message, not as part of the task run** · group: turn-cleanup-followups · `hermes_cli/harness_parts/persona/chat_turn_commit/leftover_steer.py` · `agent_runtime/chat_root_send_queue.py` · found 2026-11-12 (w1112b-hm-turn5, building `RT-STEER-NEXT-TURN-NAMES-THE-STEER-IT-DELIVERS`) · read in code, not reproduced: a `steer-next-…` turn is queued with a copy of the send of the turn the steer was typed at (`persistable_args`) with only message, ids, `new_session`, `clarify_token`, `retry_of`, `title` and `idempotency_key` replaced, so a steer typed at a Work turn runs as a turn whose `requested_by` is still `work:<request>` (and whose `max_seconds`, skills and origin are those of the Work turn) though the operator typed it · seen in a test: `tests/agent_runtime/test_chat_turn_row_says_steerable.py` shows a `requested_by work:` turn starts the steer watcher, so such a turn reads `steerable` true for that reason and not because an operator is watching a stream · guess: it is also unattended and budgeted as a Work turn, and anything that keys on the requester (not on the client message id) could count it toward the Work run; seen live by w1112b-turn6 only that it ran and answered · siblings: `RT-STEER-ONLY-STREAMING-TURNS` (closed 7df8385a42), `RT-STEER-NEXT-TURN-NAMES-THE-STEER-IT-DELIVERS` · handoff 2026-11-12 (w1112b-hm-turn5): reproduce on `tests/agent_runtime/held_model_turn.py` (a Work turn, one late steer, run the queued entry) and read the requester and budget of the next turn and the settle of the Work run; decide with the owner what a late operator steer at a Work turn should run as · seen in a test 2026-11-12 (w1112b-hm-rejoin4b, `tests/agent_runtime/test_late_steer_at_a_work_turn.py`, whole turns on nekwo-ubuntu24): reproduced. A Work turn queued as `PersonaTurnExecutor.start_from` queues it, held at the model, takes a steer that lands in no tool round; the queued `steer-next-…` send carries the Work turn's `requested_by work:<request>`, its `max_seconds` and its whole `send_origin` (`kind: work`, the request, its rows — `persistable_args` copies the Work entry's own origin over the fresh one). Run, it reads `steerable` true with no `work_request` on its `running_work` row, a steer late for IT is queued as a third turn of the same request, and at its end the Work executor reports the request ended a SECOND time as that turn (`work.exec_settle`, verdict failed — a chat reply has no work report); the request had already been reported ended on the Work turn's reply before the late message was read · read in code, not reproduced: the Work engine (`agent_runtime/work_store/engine.py` `on_settle`, record already settled) and the batch dispatcher (`agent_runtime/batch/dispatcher.py` `settled`, lane no longer running) drop that second report; a batch lane's late turn also inherits the lane's working folder and lent skills (the same copy); on a chat bound to an until-done run every turn's settle already feeds the run's after-turn hook (`PersonaTurnExecutor.settled`), so there a late steer is part of the run by design · options: (A) an ordinary chat message — the late turn is queued as the operator's own send (their requester, the chat's default budget, no Work origin, the agent's normal folder); the task card shows the task as it ended, the late message is answered in the chat like any message, and the task is reported ended once; (B) part of the task — the run is not reported ended until the late turn ends and that turn's reply decides the result; the board shows the task still running and tags the late turn to it; needs the run's ending to wait on the chat's queue, the late turn told to write a work report, and a bound on steers that keep arriving; (C) leave it and only stop the second report — the turn stays labelled as the task while changing nothing of it · Recommend: (A) for one-shot and batch-lane Work turns — the run has already ended and been judged when the late message is read, and (B) would make every lane's landing wait on chat; leave until-done runs as they are · handoff 2026-11-12 (w1112b-hm-rejoin4b): the test's assertions marked TODAY are the ones the ruled answer changes; for (A) the change is in `leftover_steer.queue_leftover_steer` and `_requeue_from_ledger` (what the queued send keeps of the send it was typed at) and `requeue_dead_turn_steers` reads the same `send_args` · done when: a test states what a late steer at a Work turn runs as, and the owner has agreed it · RULED 2026-11-12: owner "it should be an ordinary chat message" — option A for one-shot and wave-lane tasks (the late message runs as the operator's own chat turn, the task is reported ended once and its card shows it as it ended); until-done runs stay as they are. The question put was: you type a message at a running task and it arrives too late to change that run — should it run next as an ordinary chat message from you (A), or count as part of the task and be able to change the task's result (B)? - **`RT-REALM-PUBLISH-RACE-SAYS-UNREACHABLE` — A member who loses a publish race is told so and can pull and publish again, instead of being stuck behind a remote-unreachable error** · group: workspace-absorbs-projects · rewritten 2026-11-13 (audit): was "A publish that loses a race to another member says so, instead of claiming the remote is unreachable" — the title named only the wrong message; the `established` segment of this row found the loser is DEADLOCKED (publish refuses `sync_behind`, pull is fast-forward-only and aborts on the diverged commit with the same wrong code), which the done-when already covers · read in code 2026-11-13 (not reproduced): `agent_runtime/realm_sync/publish.py` `publish_realm_sync` refreshes remote tracking, refuses `sync_behind` when `behind > 0`, then stages, commits and plain-pushes in `_stage_commit_and_push` — the window between that fetch and that push is closed by nothing: no lock, no retry-with-pull, no `--force-with-lease` (rightly), so two members publishing the same realm at once leave the loser's push rejected non-fast-forward · read in code 2026-11-13 (not reproduced): the loser's rejection is caught and re-raised as `sync_remote_unreachable`, "committed locally but could not push upstream" — which is exactly the mislabel the comment at the top of `publish_realm_sync` says the fetch-first discipline exists to prevent; fetch-first NARROWS that window, it does not close it, and the comment reads as though it did · ANSWERED by the `established` segment of this row — the loser does NOT recover, and the whole-document merge this segment wondered about is never reached. As filed it read: not established 2026-11-13: whether the loser recovers by itself — its next publish would fetch, see `behind > 0` and refuse `sync_behind` "pull before publishing", and whether the pull path merges a diverged local publish commit cleanly (levels merge at whole-document granularity, so a two-sided divergence is a HOLD with a conflict sidecar) was NOT traced · why it matters now: `EterniaBackend_brain` `REALM-GIT-TOKEN-NOT-CACHED` reads the same flow for GitHub quota; one repository per server means every member of that server pushes to one branch, so this race is the normal case under load, not an edge [ruled otherwise since, 2026-11-13: one realm, one repository — launcher row `MC-REALM-REPO-PER-REALM`, backend row `REALM-GITHOST-BINDING-PER-REALM` — which narrows the racers to one realm's publishers once it is built; until then this stands] · established 2026-11-13 (review batch 14, read in code): the loser does NOT recover — it DEADLOCKS. Its next publish raises `sync_behind` "pull before publishing" (`publish.py` l.108-109), but the pull is `git pull --ff-only` (`git.py` l.230, from `pull.py` l.102), which cannot advance past the diverged local publish commit, and `_git` (`git.py` l.311-324) maps that abort to `sync_remote_unreachable` TOO — both verbs answer with the same wrong code and the whole-document merge machinery is never reached; reproduces locally with a `file://` remote, no server needed  · seen in a test 2026-11-13 (w1113-publishrace, `tests/agent_runtime/test_realm_sync_publish_race.py`, one bare remote on disk, no server, no network): the loser is deadlocked and BOTH ways out answer with the network, exactly as the `established` segment read it. Two members publish one realm, the second racing in the window between its own fetch and its own push (the other member publishes inside a patched `_refresh_remote_tracking`): the loser's push is rejected non-fast-forward and comes back `sync_remote_unreachable`; its next publish comes back `sync_behind` "pull before publishing"; its pull comes back `sync_remote_unreachable` carrying git's own "Not possible to fast-forward, aborting" in `safe_details.stderr`. Neither verb says one human word about the other publisher, and the store-level reconciliation (inbox/HOLD + conflict sidecar) is never reached · RULED 2026-11-13: owner — "yes we need to give it the same error it happens to me all the time i need to pull and fix conflicts before pushing" and "for somethings like our map metadata we should auto reconicle that but for developer stuff they have to do that" and "lets not over engineer it". So: say what git says (someone else pushed first, pull and resolve), and let the pull REACH the reconciliation that already exists — the inbox/HOLD + conflict-sidecar machinery already auto-reconciles what is safe and holds what is not, which is exactly the owner's split. Build NO new reconciliation layer, no lock, no retry loop  · done when: a push rejected for non-fast-forward reports a typed refusal saying someone else published first and to pull, NOT `sync_remote_unreachable`; the pull path reaches the existing whole-document merge instead of aborting on `--ff-only`; a test pins the two error paths apart; and nothing new is built beyond those two  · DONE 2026-11-13 c90825076d · result: seen live 2026-11-13 (w1113-publishrace, QA copy on Xvfb, serve from the lane hermes tree at c90825076d, local realm on a bare remote): losing publish -> sync_push_rejected 'Another member published to this realm first; pull before publishing again.' (exit 4); pull -> pulled, the diverged branch mer
- **`RT-REALM-SYNC-COMMIT-LEAKS-MEMBER-EMAIL` — Publishing a realm never puts a member's real name and email address into a repository they may not control** · group: workspace-absorbs-projects · `agent_runtime/realm_sync/git.py` `_ensure_git_identity` (l.418-422 on origin/main 2026-11-14, refreshed by review batch 15; filed as l.348-352), `agent_runtime/git_cmd.py` `run_git` · **seen in a probe 2026-11-13** (the function called on a fresh repository, then one commit; NOT driven through the publish verb): the robot identity `Hermes Realm Sync` / `realm-sync@localhost` is written **only when `git config user.email` / `user.name` answer nothing**. That lookup is not `--local` and `run_git` passes the caller's environment through, so on a machine whose user has a GLOBAL git identity the commit carried THAT PERSON'S OWN name and email; only with `HOME` pointed at an empty directory did it carry the robot. Nearly every developer machine has a global git identity, so the robot is the exception, not the rule · why this is its own row and not a note on `RT-REALM-SYNC-COMMITS-NAME-NOBODY`: that row WANTS attributed history and could be closed by adding attribution deliberately, which would leave this untouched. The exposure here is different in kind — an identity the member never chose to publish, written into commit metadata that is permanent, survives a repository going public, and reaches every member a server shares the repo with. `git log` and `git blame` carry it; rewriting history is the only removal · guess, not measured: the blast radius. A workspace repository is private by default with a picker (`MC-GIT-CONNECT-REPO-VISIBILITY`, Eternia), so the first exposure is to a server's other members rather than the world — but a repo made public later carries the whole history with it, and nobody re-consents · do: reproduce it through the real publish path first (this was a function-level probe, so the row's own premise is not yet proven end to end), then decide whether realm-sync forces its own identity unconditionally — reading `user.email` at all is the defect, and `git -c user.name=… -c user.email=… commit` or `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` in `run_git`'s environment both pin it without touching the caller's config — and whether anything already published needs saying to the owner · done when: a realm published from a machine WITH a global git identity produces a commit authored by the robot and not by the person, pinned by a case in `tests/` that sets a global identity and asserts the commit author · REQUIRES linux · NEW · reviewed 2026-11-14 w1114a.1 · DONE 2026-11-14 8070feda6f · RULED 2026-11-14: owner ("for the 18 go as recommended") — no history rewrite; a one-line note in the realm's sync panel only if real (non-QA) repositories turn out to carry a member's email
- **`RT-REALM-SYNC-ONE-CLONE-PER-REALM` — Two realms that back up to different GitHub repositories both work, instead of the second one failing with a remote error** · group: workspace-absorbs-projects · filed 2026-11-13 by lane w1113-realmplan while planning the per-realm chain (`EterniaLauncher/docs/mission_control/planned/REALM_GIT_HOSTING_PER_REALM_2026-11-13.md` F1, step 1) · why now: the owner ruled on 2026-11-13 that each realm gets its own repository, server realms and local realms alike (`EterniaLauncher` `MC-REALM-REPO-PER-REALM`) · read in code 2026-11-13, not reproduced: `agent_runtime/realm_sync/git.py` `_sync_repo_path` keys the clone directory by `realm.server_id or "local"`, so every realm of a server shares one working tree and every server-less realm shares one called `local`, while `Realm.sync_manifest_ref` (`agent_runtime/models.py` l.229) is already per realm — so two realms can name different remotes today but cannot have different clones · read in code 2026-11-13, not reproduced: `_adopt_credential_remote` in the same file calls it "the shared local repo" in its own docstring and moves a realm sync_manifest_ref only when that does not move the repo, and the second realm to bind a different remote is refused `url_mismatch`, which `_ADOPT_REFUSAL_CODES` maps to `sync_remote_mismatch` · read in code 2026-11-13, not reproduced: the obvious fix has one trap — `realm_sync_root() / safe_path_token(realm_id) /` is ALREADY the realm baseline directory (`agent_runtime/paths.py` l.287-583, board_baseline.json and twelve more), so keying the clone by a bare realm id asks git to clone into a directory that already holds files, which git refuses; the per-realm clone needs its own namespace under the sync root · why it is the chain head and not a parallel: every per-realm repository step in the Eternia task is unusable until this lands, because the repositories collide in one working tree whichever subject the backend binding names · done when: a realm clone is the realm own, two realms of one server bound to different repositories each publish and pull without `sync_remote_mismatch`, a server-less realm no longer shares a tree with the player other local realms, an existing clone is migrated or re-cloned without losing unpublished work, and the per-realm clone path does not collide with the realm baseline directory · reviewed 2026-11-14 w1114a.1 · DONE 2026-11-14 4d344a43bd
- **`RT-REALM-SYNC-COMMITS-NAME-NOBODY` — A realm's git history says who published each change, instead of attributing every member's work to one robot** · group: workspace-absorbs-projects · **WRONG as a general statement, corrected 2026-11-13 by an audit — the robot identity is a FALLBACK, not what every commit carries.** Seen in a probe 2026-11-13 (the function called on a fresh repository, then one commit; not driven through the publish verb): `_ensure_git_identity` (`agent_runtime/realm_sync/git.py` l.418-422 on origin/main 2026-11-14, refreshed by review batch 15; filed as l.348-352, not l.339-343) writes `Hermes Realm Sync` / `realm-sync@localhost` only when `git config user.email` / `user.name` answer nothing, that lookup is not `--local`, and `agent_runtime/git_cmd.py` `run_git` passes the caller's environment through — so on a machine whose user has a GLOBAL git identity the commit carried that person's own name and email, and only with no global identity did it carry the robot. As filed it read: read in code 2026-11-13 (not reproduced): `agent_runtime/realm_sync/git.py` `_ensure_git_identity` (l.339-343) sets EVERY realm-sync commit to `Hermes Realm Sync <realm-sync@localhost>`, for every member of every realm. So a repo's history cannot answer "who added this?" — `git log` and `git blame` name the same robot for all of it · weakened by the correction above — where a global git identity exists, the member's own name and email ARE written into the repository today, which is exactly the leak this segment credits the robot identity with preventing; so the history is a MIX (a robot on some machines, a person on others) and the privacy question is live now, not only a risk of the proposed fix. As filed it read: why it matters more than it looks: the identity is deliberate and has a real upside (it leaks no member identity into a repo the player may share, and it keeps commits stable across machines), so this row is NOT "just use the member's name" — that would publish people's identities into a repository whose visibility they do not control · where attribution DOES live today (read in code 2026-11-13): `eternia-backend/realms/services.py` `record_credential_issuance` audits actor + realm + scope at every mint. That is an Eternia-side record of who HELD a publish credential, not of who authored a given change, and matching one to the other means lining up timestamps by hand · the sharp case, and why this is filed now: a skill package that arrives through a merged pull request is auto-adopted into every member's runtime (see the skill-inbox lane), and the commit that carried it says `Hermes Realm Sync`. Asking "where did this skill come from" then has no answer inside the repo · done when: a reviewer has ruled on what attribution a workspace repo should carry — a trailer naming the publishing member's Eternia id, a mapping kept Eternia-side only, or the robot identity kept deliberately with the reason written down — and the chosen one is in place on every machine, whether or not its user has a git identity of their own · RULED 2026-11-14: owner ("for the 18 go as recommended") — commits keep the robot identity and add a commit trailer carrying the publishing member's Eternia public id; never an email or a real name — this answers the plan's privacy question · reviewed 2026-11-14 w1114a.1 · DONE 2026-11-14 c773842aa1 · handoff 2026-11-14 (w1114a-hsync): hermes half landed c773842aa1 — credential reads an optional member_public_id (UUID only) and the publish commit carries an Eternia-Member trailer; the robot stays the author (8070feda6f). Waits on the backend adding member_public_id (player.public_id) to both sync-credential payloads (realms/services.py, realms/local.py); Eternia passes the decoded map through untouched (read in code, not verified live: realm_sync_api.dart fetchSyncCredential → realm_sync_command_transport.dart jsonEncode). Then a live publish shows the trailer · handoff 2026-11-14 (w1114a-hsync): both halves in: backend d103d9b7 sends member_public_id (server, local, Studio credentials); seen via CLI 2026-11-14 from a home with a global git identity, publish with a local: credential carrying the field — the backup repo's commit is authored and committed by Hermes Realm Sync <realm-sync@localhost> with trailer Eternia-Member: <the uuid>; staging not yet deployed, so not read against staging

- **`RT-REALM-PUBLISHING-PUBLISHES-SECRETS-IN-THE-FOLDER` — A key or password file left in a folder a player publishes is not put on the public web without them seeing it first** · group: runtime-followups · from: `TASK-REALM-ARTIFACTS-REPOSITORY` · filed 2026-11-16 by lane w1115e-ph while building `RT-REALM-PUBLISHING-PUSHES-A-FOLDER` · read in code, not reproduced: `agent_runtime/realm_publishing/push.py` `publish_folder` pushes every file of the folder as it is (owner Q1: "a ready-made static folder pushed as is, no generator"), leaving out only `.git` directories; realm sync refuses secret-looking paths before its push (`realm_sync/pull.py` secret scan, `SECRET_PATH_MARKERS`) and publishing runs no such check, so a build folder holding `.env`, `*.pem` or `id_rsa` would go to a repository whose site is public whatever its visibility (plan §3) · guess: build tools rarely leave such files in their output folder · RULED 2026-11-16: owner "ah okay cool yes" — three layers: (1) Eternia's Publish action shows the dry run's exact file list and the player confirms (`MC-REALM-PUBLISHING-PUBLISH-A-FOLDER-FROM-THE-BLOCK`); (2) this verb skips hidden files (dotfiles: `.env`, `.git`, `.ssh`, …) by default and honours an ignore file in the folder; (3) it refuses, naming them, files that still look like secrets (`.env*`, `id_rsa*`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, credential files, token-shaped text) with `sync_secret_excluded`, reusing realm sync's secret predicates; secrets live in Eternia's secret store, never a repository · done when: a folder holding a dotfile publishes without it, a folder holding `id_rsa` or a token-shaped file is refused naming them and nothing is pushed, an ignore-file entry is left out, each pinned by a test with its mutation seen red, read live on a private staging test repository · review w1115e.28 (2026-11-16), read in code at f4fd825dde: the premise holds — `push.py` `_site_files` counts and `_replace_tree` copies every file but `.git` (`shutil.ignore_patterns(".git")`), so a `.env` or `id_rsa` in the folder is pushed; not churn: a public site is the one place a leaked key cannot be taken back · architecture (review w1115e.28): fork-owned, all in `agent_runtime/realm_publishing/push.py`, no `realm_sync/` verb and no upstream file; compute the file list ONCE (dotfiles and ignore-file entries left out, `.nojekyll` still added by the verb) and copy exactly that list in `_replace_tree` — today the count and the copy walk the folder separately, so a skip added to one only would publish what the dry run hid; the refusal runs before the clone is touched, dry run included, so the dry run shows it too · read in code: realm sync's predicates cover only part of the ruled list — `realm_sync/families.py` `_is_secretish_path` matches whole path parts against `SECRET_PATH_MARKERS` (`realm_sync/models.py`: `.env`, `token`, `credentials`, `secrets` …) and `realm_sync/pull.py` `_file_contains_secret_assignment` reads `SECRET_ASSIGNMENT_RE` (the token-shaped text); `.env.local`, `id_rsa*`, `*.pem` and `*.key` are in neither, so publishing adds its own name patterns beside them and imports the two predicates (lifting them to public names in `realm_sync`, which is fork-owned) — one owner each, never a copied marker set; whether realm sync should also refuse `id_rsa`/`*.pem` is not this row · added by review w1115e.28 (owner layer 1 needs it, read in code): the dry run answers only a count (`_envelope(files=len(files))`), so Eternia cannot show "the dry run's exact file list"; the `realm_publishing_push` envelope gains an additive `paths` key (the relative paths that will go up, `files` kept as the count), and `docs/agent-runtime-harness/03-transport-and-wire.md` and `tests/agent_runtime/serve_rpc_manifest.expected.json` follow; Eternia `MC-REALM-PUBLISHING-PUBLISH-A-FOLDER-FROM-THE-BLOCK` reads it · edge, documented not worked: a site that needs a dotfolder (`.well-known/`) is left out by the default skip; the ignore file's syntax (guess: gitignore-style, its own name such as `.publishignore`) is the lane's to pick and name in the wire doc · reviewed 2026-11-16 w1115e.28 · DONE 2026-11-16 60f793bda7 · result: hidden files and folders skipped, .publishignore (gitignore subset) honoured, secrets refused with sync_secret_excluded naming them (realm sync's is_secretish_path + file_contains_secret_assignment imported, plus SECRET_NAME_PATTERNS and a private-key block), dry run answers additive paths list = th

- **`RT-ANTHROPIC-WRONG-PASTE-ENDS-THE-SIGN-IN` — A wrong or mistyped Anthropic sign-in code asks for the code again, instead of failing the whole sign-in** · group: provider-api-key-and-usage-source · seen 2026-11-16 by Eternia lane w1116b-prov, reproduced offline on hermes main d15a675ebd (no network: a state mismatch aborts before the token exchange): `ProviderSignIns(spawn=run_login_in_process).begin("anthropic")` reads `awaiting_code`; `complete(login_id, "somecode#not-the-state")` answers `completing`, the next poll reads `failed` with `error_code: login_failed`, and a second `complete` is refused `login_not_awaiting_code` — one wrong paste ends the sign-in and the operator must press Sign in again · read in code: upstream `agent/anthropic_credentials.py` `run_hermes_oauth_login_pure` returns None alike on an empty paste, a state mismatch, a failed exchange and a reply with no access token; `hermes_cli/auth_commands.py` `_anthropic_oauth_login` turns None into SystemExit; `hermes_cli/provider_browser_login.py` `run_browser_login` folds every one to `login_failed` ("Sign-in did not finish."); the child's `_process_quiet` drops upstream's state-mismatch warning, and nothing in `agent_runtime/provider_signin.py` or `agent_runtime/serve_rpc/provider.py` logs a failed session's code — so no serve log can say which failure an operator hit · what it may explain (guess, not shown): the owner's "logged in first time failed second worked" (Eternia `MC-PROVIDER-SIGNIN-FIRST-TRY-FAILS`); that first failure was on another box and left no reason anywhere · fix (fork-owned seam, upstream untouched): the `_anthropic` driver compares the pasted line's `#state` with the `state` of the authorize link it already saw (`shown`) and, on a mismatch or an empty paste, emits a closed event (e.g. `{"event": "code_rejected", "code": "code_mismatch"}`) and reads another paste; `ProviderSignIns` puts the session back to `awaiting_code` with a `code_refusal` in its view; a refused or timed-out exchange ends as its own closed code (e.g. `exchange_failed`, added to `CHILD_ERROR_CODES`) instead of `login_failed`; the serve logs each failed session's closed code, never its text · done when: a registry test pastes a wrong-state code and reads `awaiting_code` with `code_refusal: code_mismatch`, a second paste is taken, and a failed exchange reads `exchange_failed` · Eternia's half waits on this one: `MC-PROVIDER-SIGNIN-FIRST-TRY-FAILS` shows the refusal beside the code field · single owner: the new closed codes live in `CHILD_ERROR_CODES`; Eternia's half (`MC-PROVIDER-SIGNIN-FIRST-TRY-FAILS`) reads them through Eternia's generated hermes mirror, never a hand copy · not churn: one wrong paste ends a user's sign-in · reviewed 2026-11-17 w1115e.36 · DONE 2026-11-19 c20aa0acdf · result: a wrong or empty paste reads awaiting_code with code_refusal (code_mismatch / code_empty) and the next paste is taken; a failed or timed-out exchange ends exchange_failed; the serve logs each failed session's closed code; fix c20aa0acdf + test ad94dcdc7c; read offline through ProviderSig

The closure-seam lever MEASURED (walk with parents over the real tree, scratch scripts): the '~10 MiB' guess is refuted — switching off tui_gateway + the CLI renderers (cli_render/cli_modern_layout/cli_subagent_monitor/banner/journey/gateway_chat_view) + plugins_cmd* + gateway.run/slash_commands/platforms/relay together drops 5.0 MB raw (~1.5 MiB compressed), and tui_gateway stays pinned by eager imports (agent_runtime.conversations.worker_skills, agent_runtime._upstream_doors, agent_runtime.gateway.contract_seam) plus ~60 lazy sites into upstream modules; no single module cut saves over 1.4 MB raw (hermes_cli is 446 modules reached through ~140 edges from run_agent/model_tools/serve_rpc). rich (+pygments 1.17) is reached through hermes_cli.config -> left_core_migration -> plugins_cmd_install (62 unguarded sites), prompt_toolkit through agent.display (73). Levers left, each named: (1) class + module docstrings ~1.0 MiB (pydantic schema / argparse readers need an audit first); (2) the worker_skills/_upstream_doors -> tui_gateway seam then the CLI-render cut, ~1.5 MiB; (3) rich/pygments/prompt_toolkit omitted_distributions once their kept sites are guarded, ~1.8 MiB; (4) locales: 17 languages' core catalogs 1.53 MiB — shipping fewer languages is an owner call. Even all four leave ~6 MiB over: the 6.8 MiB target needs an owner call (limit, or a download-on-demand engine) — left: owner decision on the target, then levers 1-3 · handoff 2026-11-19 (w1118a-hbundle3): w1118a-hbundle3 built levers (a) and (c), measured (b) and (d); android_arm64 bundled-phone, the whole bundle zipped at deflate -9 (18.86 MiB on this measure = the 18.89 above): (a) class + module docstrings emptied (1fee6ce2df; __doc__ readers audited: argparse --help takes "", pydantic's schema reader is not shipped) 18.86 -> 17.63; (c) rich, prompt-toolkit, pygments, markdown-it-py, mdurl left out behind a fork stand-in, agent_runtime/terminal_ui_stand_in.py, no upstream edit (1cd52bc54e) 17.63 -> 15.66; each build: every shipped module compiles with its source's line count, verify 0 problems. (b) measured, not built: tui_gateway.server is the phone's RPC server (worker_skills/_upstream_doors call server.handle_request), so tui_gateway cannot leave; the CLI renderers + plugins_cmd* left behind it are 0.71 MB raw (~0.2 MiB) for 68 unguarded upstream edges, the gateway.run cluster 1.94 MB raw (~0.55 MiB) for 1260 — churn against the gap. (d) the 17 languages' core catalogs are 1.53 MiB; English only saves at most ~1.45. Gap to ~6.8 MiB: 8.9 MiB; per part now (compressed/raw MiB): app/hermes_cli 2.46/9.4, app/agent_runtime 1.66/6.0, app/locales 1.53/5.2, app/agent 1.37/5.2, app/gateway 1.27/4.9, site-packages/opt (flet image libs) 1.16/2.6, dulwich 0.68/2.9, app/tools 0.62/2.2, PIL 0.60/1.7, phone_forced/tools 0.49/1.8, app/tui_gateway 0.41/1.6, wcwidth 0.27/1.6. Decision analysis — raise the limit: the 18 MiB is an unmeasured estimate (Eternia SPEC §6: 'the interpreter is most of it; Hermes code is small', refuted by measure; MC-PHONE-SIZE-BUDGET-NEVER-MEASURED calls it an estimate), store ceilings are far off (Play base download 200 MB, iOS cellular prompt 200 MB), the measured delta is about 27 MiB (runtime 11.2 + bundle 15.7), a few seconds of download, and the work is an Eternia doc edit plus a re-measure; download on demand: same bytes later at a worse moment (first agent use, maybe offline), a downloader, integrity check, engine/app version coherence and first-run states on two stores, and store policy friction on code delivered after install (Play asset packs carry no native code, so libpython stays in the base; App Review 2.5.2) — large work, worse UX; fewer languages: at most ~1.45 MiB, costs every non-English user, does not close the gap. RULED 2026-11-19: owner said "when there is a decision for this session, do an analysis on it and go with reccomended" — recommended: raise the limit, re-based on the measured delta with headroom (about 28 MiB per ABI) and kept shrink-only; this supersedes the same-day 'keep the 18 MiB, the bundle moves' ruling on Eternia MC-PHONE-ENGINE-DOWNLOAD-TWICE-ITS-SIZE-LIMIT, made before the levers were measured; the limit's owner is Eternia's RELEASE_GATES.md, so the edit and re-measure are Eternia's rows; this row's done-when (cut, gap named per part) is met · result: cut 18.86 -> 15.66 MiB arm64 (zip deflate -9): (a) class+module docstrings 48956ffb8a, (c) terminal-UI stand-in d5012e054b (the handoff's 1fee6ce2df/1cd52bc54e are these before the rebase); (b) and (d) measured, not built; RULED 2026-11-19 (go with recommended): raise the limit, re-based on the meas

- **`RT-PHONE-PROVIDER-KEY-SAVED-BY-THE-ENGINE` — Eternia can save an API key through the agent engine, so a phone with no installed service connects a provider with its own key** · group: phone-harness · from: launcher `MC-PHONE-CONNECTIONS-CANNOT-SAVE-AN-API-KEY` (filed by review w1118a.35; that row waits on this one) · fork / `agent_runtime/serve_rpc/provider.py` (a new `runtime.provider.key.set`, `TIER_CONSOLE`) · fork / `hermes_cli/auth_noninteractive.py` (`set_key`, called, not changed) · read in code (review w1118a.35): no `runtime.provider.*` method saves a key — the table is configure, dependency.install, env_token.remove, list, models, pack.activate, refresh, signin.begin/cancel/complete/poll, signout, source.set and usage, and `provider_configure` says a key-needing mode is "keyed through `auth set-key`"; `auth set-key` is the argv verb Eternia runs, which a phone with no installed service refuses (seen live by w1118a-phoneG); `set_key` already holds the whole save (the key variable lookup, the custom-pool entry, the one write choke point `credential_lifecycle.save_provider_env_credential`, an ack of names and the home only, class-name-only failures); with a bound host store (required for the embedded phone serve) the home `.env` and `auth.json` are host-store slots (`desktop_binding.HOME_SECRET_FILES`, `env_loader`/`config` fork seams), so the key lands in the phone's OS secure store, not a plaintext file · RULED 2026-11-19: owner said "when there is a decision for this session, do an analysis on it and go with reccomended" — review w1118a.35 D1: one fork-owned method, `runtime.provider.key.set`, params `provider`, `key`, optional `label` (no `profile`: the serve's own home), refusing a key that is empty, longer than a named `MAX_KEY_CHARS` or holds CR, LF or NUL (`key_invalid`) as `signin.complete` refuses a code, and answering `set_key`'s ack (provider, label, key_var, home) through `_off_reader`; `NonInteractiveAuthError` maps to a refusal by its code (`no_key_var` among them), anything else to the class-name-only failure; the key travels in the call's params like `signin.complete`'s code — on a phone that is the in-process wire, never argv, a socket or a log — and lives in one local for one call; rejected: Eternia writing the key into its own secure storage and handing the engine a slot name (a second credential owner beside the engine's store, and every reader of the pool would need the slot), and an argv shim the phone runner allows (a phone has no process to spawn) · secrets: the reply, every refusal and every failure frame carry no key; pytest sends a marker key and asserts it is absent from each frame and from captured logs; with `FakeHostSecureStore` bound the key is in its `.env` slot and no plaintext `.env` exists at the disk path · done when: the method is in the serve's advertised list; it saves an Anthropic key (the key variable written, the pool reads it) and a custom pool's key (the pool entry); an unknown provider and a provider with no key variable are refused by code; the secret checks above pass;
